382.         private UInt32 lastProcessedAdId = 0;
383.         private void metadata_emsg_mp4_CueEntered(TimedMetadataTrack timedMetadataTrack, MediaCueEventArgs args)
384.         {
385.             StringBuilder logMsg = new StringBuilder();
386.             object customValue = null;
387.             timedMetadataTrack.PlaybackItem.Source.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
388.             string contentId = (string)customValue;
389.             logMsg.AppendLine($"{contentId} mp4 emsg DataCue CueEntered raised.");
390.             var dataCue = args.Cue as DataCue;
391.             if (dataCue != null)
392.             {
393.                 // Ref: ISO/IEC 20009-1, Section 5.10.3.3.2 Definition of Box Type: 'emsg'
394.                 string scheme_id_uri = string.Empty;
395.                 string value = string.Empty;
396.                 UInt32 timescale = (UInt32) TimeSpan.TicksPerSecond;
397.                 UInt32 presentation_time_delta = (UInt32)dataCue.StartTime.Ticks;
398.                 UInt32 event_duration = (UInt32)dataCue.Duration.Ticks;
399.                 UInt32 id = 0;
400.                 Byte[] message_data = null;
401. 
402.                 const string scheme_id_uri_key = "emsg:scheme_id_uri";
403.                 object propValue = null;
404.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(scheme_id_uri_key, out propValue);
405.                 scheme_id_uri = propValue!=null ? (string)propValue : "";
406. 
407.                 const string value_key = "emsg:value";
408.                 propValue = null;
409.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(value_key, out propValue);
410.                 value = propValue != null ? (string)propValue : "";
411. 
412.                 const string timescale_key = "emsg:timescale";
413.                 propValue = null;
414.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(timescale_key, out propValue);
415.                 timescale = propValue != null ? (UInt32)propValue : timescale;
416. 
417.                 const string presentation_time_delta_key = "emsg:presentation_time_delta";
418.                 propValue = null;
419.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(presentation_time_delta_key, out propValue);
420.                 presentation_time_delta = propValue != null ? (UInt32)propValue : presentation_time_delta;
421. 
422.                 const string event_duration_key = "emsg:event_duration";
423.                 propValue = null;
424.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(event_duration_key, out propValue);
425.                 event_duration = propValue != null ? (UInt32)propValue : event_duration;
426. 
427.                 const string id_key = "emsg:id";
428.                 propValue = null;
429.                 dataCue.Properties.TryGetValue(id_key, out propValue);
430.                 id = propValue != null ? (UInt32)propValue : 0;
431. 
432.                 logMsg.AppendLine($"Label: {timedMetadataTrack.Label}, Id: {dataCue.Id}, StartTime: {dataCue.StartTime}, Duration: {dataCue.Duration}");
433.                 logMsg.Append($"scheme_id_uri: {scheme_id_uri}, value: {value}, timescale: {timescale}, presentation_time_delta: {presentation_time_delta}, event_duration: {event_duration}, id: {id}");
434. 
435.                 if (dataCue.Data != null)
436.                 {
437.                     var dr = DataReader.FromBuffer(dataCue.Data);
438. 
439.                     // Check if this is a SCTE ad message:
440.                     // Ref:  http://dashif.org/identifiers/event-schemes/
441.                     if (scheme_id_uri.ToLower() == "urn:scte:scte35:2013:xml")
442.                     {
443.                         // SCTE recommends publishing emsg more than once, so we avoid reprocessing the same message id:
444.                         if (lastProcessedAdId != id)
445.                         {
446.                             lastProcessedAdId = id; // TODO: Requires robustness. Messages may be out of order, which would break this sample's logic.
447. 
448.                             dr.UnicodeEncoding = Windows.Storage.Streams.UnicodeEncoding.Utf8;
449.                             var scte35payload = dr.ReadString(dataCue.Data.Length);
450.                             logMsg.Append($", message_data: {scte35payload}");
451.                             ScheduleAdFromScte35Payload(timedMetadataTrack, presentation_time_delta, timescale, event_duration, scte35payload);
452.                         }
453.                         else
454.                         {
455.                             logMsg.AppendLine($"This emsg.Id, {id}, has already been processed.");
456.                         }
457.                     }
458.                     else
459.                     {
460.                         message_data = new byte[dataCue.Data.Length];
461.                         dr.ReadBytes(message_data);
462.                         // TODO: Use the 'emsg' bytes for something useful.
463.                         logMsg.Append($", message_data.Length: {message_data.Length}");
464.                     }
465.                 }
466.             }
467.             Log(logMsg.ToString());
468.         }

234. 
235.             // Register for events
236.             mp.SourceChanged += PositionUpdateControl_SourceChanged;
237.             mp.PlaybackSession.PositionChanged += PositionUpdateControl_PositionChanged;
238.             mp.PlaybackSession.SeekableRangesChanged += PositionUpdateControl_SeekableRangesChanged;
239.         }

245.         }

260.         private void UpdateSeekableRangesControl(IReadOnlyList<MediaTimeRange> SeekableRanges)
261.         {
262.             if (SeekableRanges.Count > 0)
263.             {
264.                 var SeekableRangeMin = SeekableRanges.First();
265.                 m_PlaybackPositionMinTime = SeekableRangeMin.Start;
266. 
267.                 var SeekableRangeMax = SeekableRanges.Last();
268.                 m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime = SeekableRangeMax.End;
269.             }
270.             else
271.             {
272.                 m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime = TimeSpan.FromHours(1);
273.                 m_PlaybackPositionMinTime = TimeSpan.Zero;
274.             }
275. 
276.             // TODO[migrate-018]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
277.             var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
278.             {
279.                 StartPosition.Text = m_PlaybackPositionMinTime.ToString("c");
280.                 EndPosition.Text = m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime.ToString("c");
281.             });
282. 
283.             string ranges = "";
284.             foreach (var range in SeekableRanges)
285.             {
286.                 ranges += $"[{range.Start},{range.End}],";
287.             }
288.             ranges = ranges.TrimEnd(',');
289.             Log($"MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SeekableRangesChanged: {ranges}");
290.         }

292.         private async void PositionUpdateControl_PositionChanged(MediaPlaybackSession sender, object args)

295.             var newSliderValue = GetSliderPositionAtPlaybackPosition(postion, m_SliderFullScale, m_PlaybackPositionMinTime, m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime);
296.             // TODO[migrate-019]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
297.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
298.             {

300.                 PositionSlider.Value = newSliderValue;

306.         private void SeekToRequestedPosition(TimeSpan requestedPosition)
307.         {
308.             Log($"Seeking to:{requestedPosition.ToString("c")}");
309.             mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position = requestedPosition;
310.         }
311. 
312.         #endregion
313. 
314.         /// <summary>
315.         /// We will use slider ticks to drive a playback Position.
316.         ///
317.         /// This is a linear interpolation, so:
318.         /// y = m*x + b
319.         /// Where:
320.         /// Position = m*sliderPosition + b
321.         ///
322.         /// Where we have some data points:
323.         ///
324.         /// At MinPosition, we are at slider position=0
325.         /// MinPosition = m * 0 + b
326.         /// Thus:
327.         ///   b = MinPosition
328.         ///
329.         /// We choose a 100000 tick granularity for the slider,
330.         /// at which point it will be at MaxPosition:
331.         /// MaxPosition = m * 100000 + b
332.         /// MaxPosition = m * 100000 + MinPosition
333.         ///
334.         /// Thus:
335.         ///   m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / 100000
336.         ///   m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale
337.         ///
338.         /// And more generally, we have:
339.         ///
340.         /// PlaybackPosition =  [(MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale] * SliderPosition + MinPosition
341.         /// or:
342.         /// PlaybackPosition =  ((MaxPosition - MinPosition) * SliderPosition / sliderFullScale)   + MinPosition
343.         ///
344.         /// </summary>
345.         public TimeSpan GetPlaybackPosition(double sliderPosition, double sliderFullScale, TimeSpan MinPosition, TimeSpan MaxPosition)
346.         {
347.             if (sliderFullScale == 0)
348.                 return TimeSpan.Zero;
349.             return TimeSpan.FromTicks((long)((MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks * sliderPosition / sliderFullScale) + MinPosition.Ticks);
350.         }
351. 
352.         /// <summary>
353.         /// Given:
354.         /// y = m * x + b
355.         /// x = (y - b) /m
356.         ///
357.         /// Where from above:
358.         ///  b = MinPosition
359.         ///  m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale
360.         ///
361.         /// We have:
362.         ///  Position = (PlaybackPosition - MinPosition) * sliderFullScale / (MaxPosition - MinPosition)
363.         ///
364.         /// </summary>
365.         public double GetSliderPositionAtPlaybackPosition(TimeSpan playbackPosition, double sliderFullScale, TimeSpan MinPosition, TimeSpan MaxPosition)
366.         {
367.             if ((MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks == 0)
368.                 return 0;
369. 
370.             if (playbackPosition == TimeSpan.Zero)
371.             {
372.                 LoggerControl.Log("Transient playbackPosition == TimeSpan.Zero", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
373.             }
374. 
375.             var interpolatedPosition = (playbackPosition - MinPosition).Ticks * sliderFullScale / (MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks;
376. 
377.             if (interpolatedPosition < 0)
378.             {
379.                 interpolatedPosition = 0;
380.                 LoggerControl.Log("Transient calculatedPosition < 0", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
381.             }
382. 
383.             return interpolatedPosition;
384.         }
385. 
386.         #endregion
387. 
388. 
389.         #region DiscreteControls
390. 
391.         private void SeekFixedAmount_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
392.         {
393.             TimeSpan amount = TimeSpan.Zero;
394.             string selected = (sender as Button).Tag.ToString();
395.             TimeSpan.TryParse(selected, out amount);
396.             if (amount != TimeSpan.Zero)
397.             {
398.                 var requestedPosition = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position + amount;
399.                 SeekToRequestedPosition(requestedPosition);
400.             }
401.         }

465.         {

467.

469.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
470.             {
471.                 switch (sender.PlaybackState)
472.                 {
473.                     case MediaPlaybackState.None:
474.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Opening:
475.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Buffering:
476.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = false;
477.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = false;
478.                         break;
479.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Playing:
480.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = false;
481.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = true;
482.                         break;
483.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Paused:
484.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = true;
485.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = false;
486.                         break;
487.                     default:
488.                         break;
489.                 }
490.             });
491.         }

80.         {

99.         {
100.             logView.Log($"item.AudioTracksChanged: CollectionChange:{args.CollectionChange}, Index:{args.Index}, Total:{sender.AudioTracks.Count}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
101.

103.             {
104.                 case CollectionChange.Reset:
105.                     foreach (AudioTrack track in sender.AudioTracks)
106.                     {

108.                         // This occurs prior to the track media being opened.
109.                         // Register here so that we can receive the Track.OpenFailed event.
110.                         track.OpenFailed += AudioTrack_OpenFailed;
111.                     }
112.                     break;
113.                 case CollectionChange.ItemInserted:
114.                     // Additional tracks added after loading the main source should be registered here.
115.                     AudioTrack newTrack = sender.AudioTracks[(int)args.Index];
116.                     newTrack.OpenFailed += AudioTrack_OpenFailed;
117.                     break;
118.             }

72.         {
73.             // Get the MediaPlaybackItem that corresponds to the MediaSource.
74.             MediaPlaybackItem item = MediaPlaybackItem.FindFromMediaSource(sender);
75.             if (item != null && item.AudioTracks.Count > 0)
76.             {
77.                 // The MediaPlaybackItem contains additional information about the underlying media.
78.                 string audioCodec = item.AudioTracks[item.AudioTracks.SelectedIndex].GetEncodingProperties().Subtype;
79. 
80.                 // We can read values from the MediaSource.CustomProperties.
81.                 object customValue = null;
82.                 sender.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("uri", out customValue);
83.                 string uri = (string)customValue;
84.                 customValue = null;
85.                 sender.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
86.                 string contentId = (string)customValue;
87.                 logView.Log($"Opened Media Source with Uri: {uri}, ContentId: {contentId}, Codec: {audioCodec}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
88. 
89.                 // This extension method in MediaPlaybackItemStringExtensions dumps all the properties from all the tracks.
90.                 var allProperties = item.ToFormattedString();
91.                 logView.Log($"MediaPlaybackItem nested properties: {allProperties}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
92.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource can manage multiple video tracks internally,
93.                 // but only a single video track is exposed in the MediaPlaybackItem, not a collection.
94.             }

26. 
27.         private LogView logView;
28.         private IHttpFilter innerFilter;
29.         private Stopwatch timeToResponseComplete;
30. 
31.         public AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LogView _logView, IHttpFilter _innerFilter)
32.         {
33.             if (_logView == null)
34.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
35.             this.logView = _logView;
36. 
37.             if (_innerFilter == null)
38.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_innerFilter));
39.             this.innerFilter = _innerFilter;
40. 
41.             this.timeToResponseComplete = new Stopwatch();
42.         }
43. 
44.         public void Dispose()
45.         {
46. 
47.             this.timeToResponseComplete = null;
48.             this.logView = null;
49.             this.innerFilter = null;
50.         }
51.         
52.         #endregion
53. 
54.         public IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> SendRequestAsync(HttpRequestMessage request)
55.         {
56.             timeToResponseComplete.Start();
57.             logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER SendRequestAsync\":{{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
58. 
59.             return AsyncInfo.Run<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(async (cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter) =>
60.             {
61.                 IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> innerOperation = innerFilter.SendRequestAsync(request);
62. 
63.                 innerOperation.Progress += new AsyncOperationProgressHandler<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(
64.                     (IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> asyncInfo, HttpProgress httpProgressInfo) =>
65.                 {
66.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Progress Handler\": {{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\", {httpProgressInfo.ToJsonObject()}}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
67.                 });
68. 
69.                 HttpResponseMessage response = await innerOperation.AsTask(cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter);
70.                 cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
71.                 // At this point, SendRequest has completed and we have a response.
72.                 timeToResponseComplete.Stop();
73. 
74.                 // We could inspect the content of the response headers:
75.                 var countHeaders = response.Headers.Count();
76.                 logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Complete\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
77. 
78.                 if(!response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
79.                 {
80.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Failed\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
81.                 }
82. 
83.                 // Note that we should NOT attempt to access the response.Content in an IHttpFilter such as this,
84.                 // the Content stream is for the base filter and AdaptiveMediaSource to use.
85.                 //
86.                 // If you want to download content in the App and then pass it to the AdaptiveMediaSource, 
87.                 // see AppDownloadedKeyRequest in Scenario3, which uses Deferral objects to manage thread timing
88.                 // with the AdaptiveMediaSource.
89.                 return response;
90.             });
91.         }
92. 
93.     }
94. }

83.                     sb.AppendLine(encodingProps.PixelAspectRatio.ToFormattedString("PixelAspectRatio"));

85.                     sb.AppendLine("StereoscopicVideoPackingMode:\t" + encodingProps.StereoscopicVideoPackingMode);
86.                     sb.AppendLine("Width:\t" + encodingProps.Width);

88.                     sb.AppendLine(additionalProperties.ToFormattedString());

145.         static string ToFormattedString(this MediaRatio mediaRatio, string key)
146.         {
147.             string retVal = string.Empty;
148.             if (!String.IsNullOrEmpty(key) && mediaRatio != null)
149.             {
150.                 retVal = key + ":\t" + mediaRatio.Numerator + "/" + mediaRatio.Denominator;
151.             }
152.             return retVal;
153.         }
154. 
155.         static string ToFormattedString(this MediaPropertySet mediaPropertySet)
156.         {
157.             var retVal = new StringBuilder();
158. 
159.             if (mediaPropertySet.Count != 0)
160.                 retVal.AppendLine("Additional Encoding Properties:");
161.             foreach (var prop in mediaPropertySet)
162.             {
163.                 // We can also get the various MF_ Guid-based props, some of which are a repeat from above:
164.                 retVal.AppendLine(MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Key) + ":\t" + MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Value));
165. 
166.                 // Special case for Byte Arrays:
167.                 if (prop.Value.GetType() == typeof(System.Byte[]))
168.                 {
169.                     var propBytes = prop.Value as System.Byte[];
170.                     if (propBytes != null && propBytes.Length > 0)
171.                     {
172.                         String byteString = "";
173.                         foreach (var b in propBytes)
174.                         {
175.                             byteString += String.Format("{0:X2} ", b);
176.                         }
177.                         retVal.AppendLine(MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Key) + " as Hex:\t" + byteString);
178.                     }
179.                 }
180.             }
181.             return retVal.ToString();
182.         }

17.     /// <summary>
18.     /// Provides very basic Json formatting support to the string extensions.
19.     /// </summary>
20.     public class JsonBuilder
21.     {
22.         private JsonObject container = new JsonObject();
23.         private JsonObject o = new JsonObject();
24. 
25.         public JsonBuilder(string className)
26.         {
27.             container.Add(className, o);
28.         }
29. 
30.         public void AddNumber(string key, double? value)
31.         {
32.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateNumberValue(value.Value));
33.         }
34. 
35.         public void AddBoolean(string key, bool? value)
36.         {
37.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateBooleanValue(value.Value));
38.         }
39. 
40.         public void AddString(string key, object value)
41.         {
42.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.ToString()));
43.         }
44. 
45.         public void AddTimeSpan(string key, TimeSpan? value)
46.         {
47.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.ToString()));
48.         }
49. 
50.         public void AddDateTime(string key, DateTimeOffset? value)
51.         {
52.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.Value.ToString("u")));
53.         }
54. 
55.         public void AddJsonValue(string key, IJsonValue value)
56.         {
57.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : value);
58.         }
59. 
60.         public JsonObject GetJsonObject()
61.         {
62.             return container;
63.         }
64. 
65.         public override string ToString()
66.         {
67.             return container.ToString();
68.         }
69.     }

23.         public static JsonObject ToJsonObject(this CurrentMediaPlaybackItemChangedEventArgs c)
24.         {
25.             var builder = new JsonBuilder("CurrentMediaPlaybackItemChangedEventArgs");
26.             builder.AddJsonValue("NewItem", c.NewItem?.ToJsonObject());
27.             builder.AddJsonValue("OldItem", c.OldItem?.ToJsonObject());
28.             builder.AddString("Reason", c.Reason);
29.             return builder.GetJsonObject();
30.         }

34.         /// </summary>
35.         public static JsonObject ToJsonObject(this MediaPlaybackItemFailedEventArgs f)

39.             builder.AddJsonValue("Item", f.Item?.ToJsonObject());

13. using System;
14. using System.Collections.Generic;
15. 
16. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions
17. {
18.     public static class MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions
19.     {
20.         // Translation of selected GUIDs used in MF to their human-readable attribute strings
21.         // These are in defined the Windows SDK and in various regkeys.
22.         // Ref: https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/desktop/dd317906(v=vs.85).aspx
23. 
24.         public static string ToMFAttributeName(Guid guidToFind)
25.         {
26.             string retVal = guidToFind.ToString();
27.             string mfAttribute;
28.             if (MfGuidDictionary.TryGetValue(guidToFind, out mfAttribute))
29.             {
30.                 retVal = mfAttribute;
31.             }
32.             return retVal;
33.         }
34.         public static string ToMFAttributeName(object objectToCheck)
35.         {
36.             // Return .ToString as default:
37.             string retVal = objectToCheck.ToString();
38. 
39.             // Passed object is Guid
40.             if (objectToCheck is Guid)
41.             {
42.                 Guid guidToFind = (Guid)objectToCheck;
43.                 retVal = ToMFAttributeName(guidToFind);
44.             }
45.             else
46.             {
47.                 // Passed object is Guid.ToString()
48.                 Guid guidToFind;
49.                 if (Guid.TryParse(retVal, out guidToFind))
50.                 {
51.                     retVal = ToMFAttributeName(guidToFind);
52.                 }
53.             }
54.             return retVal;
55.         }
56. 
57.

23.     {
24.         public const string FEATURE_NAME = "AdaptiveStreaming";
25. 
26.         List<Scenario> scenarios = new List<Scenario>
27.         {
28.             new Scenario() { Title="Simplest Adaptive Streaming", ClassType=typeof(Scenario1_SimplestAdaptiveStreaming)},
29.             new Scenario() { Title="Event Handlers", ClassType=typeof(Scenario2_EventHandlers)},
30.             new Scenario() { Title="Network Request Modification", ClassType=typeof(Scenario3_RequestModification)},
31.             new Scenario() { Title="Adaptive Streaming Tuning", ClassType=typeof(Scenario4_Tuning)},
32.             new Scenario() { Title="Metadata", ClassType=typeof(Scenario5_Metadata)},
33.             new Scenario() { Title="Ad Insertion", ClassType=typeof(Scenario6_AdInsertion)},
34.             new Scenario() { Title="Live Seekable Range", ClassType=typeof(Scenario7_LiveSeekableRange)},
35.         };
36. 
37.         public static IReadOnlyList<AdaptiveContentModel> ContentManagementSystemStub = AdaptiveContentModel.GetKnownAzureMediaServicesModels();
38.         public static AdaptiveContentModel FindContentById(int id)
39.         {
40.             return ContentManagementSystemStub.First(model => model.Id == id);
41.         }
42.     }
43. 
44.     public class Scenario
45.     {
46.         public string Title { get; set; }
47.         public Type ClassType { get; set; }
48.     }
49. 
50.     public class BitrateHelper
51.     {
52.         // Each bitrate is assigned a symbol as follows:
53.         // The bottom quartile gets 1 bar, the second quartile gets 2 bars,
54.         // the third quartile gets 3 bars, and the top quartile gets 4 bars.
55.         private Dictionary<uint, Symbol> symbolAssignment = new Dictionary<uint, Symbol>();
56. 
57.         public BitrateHelper(IEnumerable<uint> availableBitRates)
58.         {
59.             uint[] sortedBitRates = availableBitRates.ToArray();
60.             Array.Sort(sortedBitRates);
61.             int i = 0;
62.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 1 / 4)
63.             {
64.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.OneBar);
65.             }
66.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 2 / 4)
67.             {
68.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.TwoBars);
69.             }
70.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 3 / 4)
71.             {
72.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.ThreeBars);
73.             }
74.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 4 / 4)
75.             {
76.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.FourBars);
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         public Symbol GetBitrateSymbol(uint currentBitrate)
81.         {
82.             Symbol symbol = Symbol.ZeroBars;
83.             if (symbolAssignment.TryGetValue(currentBitrate, out symbol))
84.             {
85.                 return symbol;
86.             }
87.             return Symbol.ZeroBars;
88.         }
89.     }
90. }

46.             Func<Uri, HttpClient, Task<MediaPlaybackItem>> loadSourceFromUriAsync)
47.         {
48.             if (mediaPlayer == null)
49.                 throw new ArgumentNullException("mediaPlayer");
50.             this.mediaPlayer = mediaPlayer;
51. 
52.             if (adaptiveContentModels == null)
53.                 throw new ArgumentNullException("adaptiveContentModels");
54.             this.adaptiveContentModels = adaptiveContentModels;
55. 
56.             if (loggerControl == null)
57.                 throw new ArgumentNullException("loggerControl");
58.             this.loggerControl = loggerControl;
59. 
60.             if (loadSourceFromUriAsync == null)
61.                 throw new ArgumentNullException("loadSourceFromUriAsync");
62.             this.CreateMediaPlaybackItem = loadSourceFromUriAsync;
63. 
64.             this.optionalHttpClient = optionalHttpClient;
65. 
66.             // Set data context:
67.             SelectedContent.ItemsSource = this.adaptiveContentModels;
68.             // Set default item:
69.             SetSelectedModel(this.adaptiveContentModels.First());
70.         }
71. 
72.         public void SetSelectedModel(AdaptiveContentModel model)
73.         {
74.             SelectedModel = model;
75.             SelectedContent.SelectedItem = SelectedModel;
76.         }
77. 
78.         public async void HideLoadUri()
79.         {
80.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
81.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
82.             {
83.                 this.LoadUriPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
84.             });
85.         }
86.         public async void SetAutoPlay(bool IsChecked)
87.         {
88.             // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
89.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
90.             {
91.                 AutoPlay.IsChecked = IsChecked;
92.             });
93.         }
94. 
95.         private async void LoadId_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
96.         {
97.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
98.                 return;
99. 
100.             this.SelectedModel = (AdaptiveContentModel)SelectedContent.SelectedItem;
101. 
102.             UriBox.Text = SelectedModel.ManifestUri.ToString();
103.             await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(SelectedModel.ManifestUri);
104.         }
105. 
106.         private async void LoadUri_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
107.         {
108.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
109.                 return;
110. 
111.             Uri uri;
112.             if (!Uri.TryCreate(UriBox.Text, UriKind.Absolute, out uri))
113.             {
114.                 loggerControl.Log("Malformed Uri in Load text box.", SDKTemplate.Logging.LogViewLoggingLevel.Critical);
115.                 return;
116.             }
117.             await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(uri);
118.         }
119. 
120.         private async Task CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(Uri uri)
121.         {
122.             SetSourceEnabled(false);
123.             this.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
124.             this.MediaPlaybackItem = await CreateMediaPlaybackItem(uri, optionalHttpClient);
125.             if (this.MediaPlaybackItem != null)
126.             {
127.                 loggerControl.Log($"Loaded Uri: {uri}");
128.                 SetSourceEnabled(true);
129.             }
130.         }
131. 
132.         private async void SetSourceEnabled(bool SetEnabled)
133.         {
134.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
135.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
136.             {
137.                 SetSource.IsEnabled = SetEnabled;
138.             });
139.         }
140. 
141.         private void AutoPlay_Checked(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
142.         {
143.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
144.                 return;
145. 
146.             mediaPlayer.AutoPlay = (bool)(sender as CheckBox).IsChecked;
147.         }
148. 
149.         private void ForceEquirectangularSphericalVideoFrameFormat()
150.         {
151.             if (mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.None ||
152.                 mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.Opening)
153.                 return;
154. 
155.             var mpsvp = mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SphericalVideoProjection;
156.             if (mpsvp == null)
157.                 return;
158. 
159.             if (mpsvp.FrameFormat != Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular)
160.                 mpsvp.FrameFormat = Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular;
161. 
162.             if (mpsvp.IsEnabled != true)
163.                 mpsvp.IsEnabled = true;
164. 
165.             mpsvp.HorizontalFieldOfViewInDegrees = 120;
166.         }
167. 
168.         private void SetSource_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
169.         {
170.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
171.                 return;
172.             
173.             // It is at this point that the MediaSource (within a MediaPlaybackItem) will be fully resolved.
174.             // For an AdaptiveMediaSource, this is the point at which media is first requested and parsed.
175.             mediaPlayer.Source = this.MediaPlaybackItem;
176.         }
177.     }

29.     /// See the README.md for discussion of this scenario.
30.     public sealed partial class Scenario2_EventHandlers : Page
31.     {
32.         private BitrateHelper bitrateHelper;
33. 
34.         public Scenario2_EventHandlers()
35.         {
36.             this.InitializeComponent();
37.         }
38. 
39.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
42.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
43.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
44.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
45.             {
46.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
47.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
48. 
49.                 UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);
50. 
51.                 mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
52.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
53.             }
54.         }
55. 
56.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
57.         {
58.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
59.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
60.             if (mpItem != null)
61.             {
62.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
63.             }
64.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
65.             if (source != null)
66.             {
67.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
68.             }
69. 
70.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
71.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
72. 
73.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
74.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
75. 
76.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
77.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
78. 
79.             UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
80.         }
81. 
82.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             // Explicitly create the instance of MediaPlayer if you need to register for its events
85.             // (like MediaOpened / MediaFailed) prior to setting an IMediaPlaybackSource.
86.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
87. 
88.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
89.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
90. 
91.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
92.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
93.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
94.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // This Scenario focuses on event handling, mostly via the "Logger" helper classes
97.             // in the Shared folder.
98.             //
99.             // In addition, the App can also insert an IHttpFilter into the Windows.Web.Http stack
100.             // which is used by the AdaptiveMediaSource.  To do so, it must derive a class from
101.             // IHttpFilter, provide the HttpBaseProtocolFilter to that class to execute requests,
102.             // then use the derived filter as the constructor for the HttpClient.
103.             //
104.             // The HttpClient is then used in the constructor of the AdaptiveMediaSource.
105.             // The App should explicitly set:
106.             //   CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache
107.             // When passing an HttpClient into the constructor of AdpativeMediaSource,
108.             // set this on the HttpBaseProtocolFilter.
109.             //
110.             // More than one IHttpFilter can be nested, for example, an App might
111.             // use a filter to modify web requests: see Scenario3 for details.
112.             //
113.             // In this scenario, we add an AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger to provide additional
114.             // verbose logging details for each request.
115.             var baseProtocolFilter = new HttpBaseProtocolFilter();
116.             baseProtocolFilter.CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache;  // Always set WriteBehavior = NoCache
117.             var fistLevelFilter = new AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LoggerControl, baseProtocolFilter);
118.             var httpClient = new HttpClient(fistLevelFilter);
119. 
120. 
121.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
122.                 mediaPlayer,
123.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
124.                 httpClient,
125.                 LoggerControl,
126.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
127.         }
128. 
129. 
130.         #region Content Loading
131. 
132.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
133.         {
134.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
135. 
136.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
137.             if (httpClient != null)
138.             {
139.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
140.             }
141.             else
142.             {
143.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
144.             }
145. 
146.             MediaSource source;
147.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
148.             {
149.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
150. 
151.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
152.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
153. 
154.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
155.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
156. 
157.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
158.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
159. 
160.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource chooses initial playback and download bitrates.
161.                 // See the Tuning scenario for examples of customizing these bitrates.
162.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
163.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
164. 
165.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
166. 
167.             }
168.             else
169.             {
170.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
171. 
172.                 // In this scenario, we make a second attempt to load any URI that is
173.                 // not adaptive streaming as a progressive download URI:
174.                 Log($"Attempting to create a MediaSource from uri: {uri}");
175.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromUri(uri);
176.             }
177. 
178.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
179.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
180. 
181.             // You can save additional information in the CustomPropertySet for future retrieval.
182.             // Note: MediaSource.CustomProperties is a ValueSet and therefore can store
183.             // only serializable types.
184. 
185.             // Save the original Uri.
186.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
187. 
188.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
189.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
190. 
191.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
192. 
193.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
194.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
195. 
196.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
197. 
198.             return mpItem;
199.         }
200. 
201.         private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
202.         {
203.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
204.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
205.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
206.             {
207.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
208.             });
209.         }

215.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
216.         {
217.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;
218.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged += PlaybackBitrateChanged;
219.         }
220. 
221.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
222.         {
223.             if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
224.             {
225.                 adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
226.                 adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
227.             }
228.         }
229.         private async void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
230.         {
231.             uint downloadBitrate = args.NewValue;
232.             await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(downloadBitrate);
233.         }
234. 
235.         private async Task UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(uint downloadBitrate)
236.         {
237.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
238.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
239.             {
240.                 iconDownloadBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(downloadBitrate);
241.                 txtDownloadBitrate.Text = downloadBitrate.ToString();
242.             }));
243.         }
244. 
245.         private async void PlaybackBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourcePlaybackBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
246.         {
247.             uint playbackBitrate = args.NewValue;
248.             await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(playbackBitrate);
249.         }
250. 
251.         private async Task UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(uint playbackBitrate)
252.         {
253.             // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
254.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
255.             {
256.                 iconPlaybackBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(playbackBitrate);
257.                 txtPlaybackBitrate.Text = playbackBitrate.ToString();
258.             }));
259.         }

103.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
104.                 null,
105.                 LoggerControl,
106.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
107. 
108.             // There is no InboundBitsPerSecondChanged event, so we start a polling thread to update UI.
109.             PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh);
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(CancellationTokenSource cts)
113.         {
114.             ulong InboundBitsPerSecondLast = 0;
115.             var refreshRate = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(2);
116.             while (!cts.IsCancellationRequested)
117.             {
118.                 if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
119.                 {
120.                     if (InboundBitsPerSecondLast != adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond)
121.                     {
122.                         InboundBitsPerSecondLast = adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond;
123.                         InboundBitsPerSecondText.Text = InboundBitsPerSecondLast.ToString();
124.                     }
125.                 }
126.                 await Task.Delay(refreshRate);
127.             }
128.         }
129. 
130. 
131.         #region Content Loading
132. 
133.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
134.         {
135.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
136. 
137.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
138.             if (httpClient != null)
139.             {
140.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
141.             }
142.             else
143.             {
144.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
145.             }
146. 
147.             MediaSource source;
148.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
149.             {
150.                 adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
151. 
152.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
153.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
154. 
155.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
156.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
157. 
158.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
159.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
160.                 InitializeBitrateLists(adaptiveMediaSource);
161.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
162.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
163. 
164.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
165.             }
166.             else
167.             {
168.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
169.                 return null;
170.             }
171. 
172.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
173.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
174. 
175.             // Save the original Uri.
176.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
177. 
178.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
179.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
180. 
181.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
182. 
183.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
184.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
185. 
186.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
187. 
188.             return mpItem;
189.         }
190.

192.         {
193.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
194.             // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
195.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
196.             {
197.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
198.             });
199.         }

203.         #region Adaptive Bitrate control
204. 
205.         private void InitializeBitrateLists(AdaptiveMediaSource aMS)
206.         {
207.             var sortedBitrates = aMS.AvailableBitrates.OrderByDescending(br => br).Select(br => new BitrateItem(br)).ToArray();
208.             InitialBitrateList.ItemsSource = sortedBitrates;
209.             var selected = sortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
210.             InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = sortedBitrates.FirstOrDefault(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
211. 
212.             var nullableSortedBitrates = (new BitrateItem[] { new BitrateItem(null) }).Concat(sortedBitrates).ToArray();
213.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.ItemsSource = DesiredMinBitrateList.ItemsSource = nullableSortedBitrates;
214. 
215.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMaxBitrate);
216.             DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMinBitrate);
217.         }
218. 
219.         // An argument exception will be thrown if the following constraint is not met:
220.         //   DesiredMinBitrate <= InitialBitrate <= DesiredMaxBitrate
221.         private bool IsValidBitrateCombination(uint? desiredMinBitrate, uint? desiredMaxBitrate, uint initialBitrate)
222.         {
223.             // The ">" operator returns false if either operand is null. We take advantage of this
224.             // by testing in this manner. Do NOT "optimize" this by reversing the sense of the test
225.             // to "return desiredMinBitrate <= initialBitrate && initialBitrate <= desiredMaxBitrate;"
226.             if (desiredMinBitrate > initialBitrate || initialBitrate > desiredMaxBitrate)
227.             {
228.                 return false;
229.             }
230.             return true;
231.         }
232. 
233.         private void InitialBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
234.         {
235.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
236.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
237.             {
238.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, selection.Bitrate.Value))
239.                 {
240.                     adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate = selection.Bitrate.Value;
241.                 }
242.                 else
243.                 {
244.                     InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
245.                 }
246.             }
247.         }
248. 
249.         private void DesiredMinBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
250.         {
251.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
252.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
253.             {
254.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
255.                 {
256.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
257.                 }
258.                 else
259.                 {
260.                     DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
261.                 }
262.             }
263.         }
264. 
265.         private void DesiredMaxBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
266.         {
267.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
268.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
269.             {
270.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
271.                 {
272.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
273.                 }
274.                 else
275.                 {
276.                     DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
277.                 }
278.             }
279.         }

364.     /// <summary>
365.     /// Item which provides a nicer display name for "null" bitrate.
366.     /// </summary>
367.     class BitrateItem
368.     {
369.         public uint? Bitrate { get; private set; }
370.         public BitrateItem(uint? bitrate)
371.         {
372.             Bitrate = bitrate;
373.         }
374. 
375.         public override string ToString()
376.         {
377.             return (Bitrate == null) ? "Not set" : Bitrate.ToString();
378.         }
379.     }

427.         /// </summary>
428.         /// <param name="protection">Protection level to use when imposing bandwidth restriction.</param>
429.         /// <param name="ams">AdaptiveMediaSource on which to impose restrictions</param>
430.         private async void SetMaxBitrateForProtectionLevel(HdcpProtection? protection, AdaptiveMediaSource ams)
431.         {
432.             // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
433.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>

435.                 EffectiveHdcpProtectionText.Text = protection.ToString();

438.                 {
439.                     // Get a sorted list of available bitrates.
440.                     var bitrates = new List<uint>(ams.AvailableBitrates);
441.                     bitrates.Sort();
442. 
443.                     // Apply maximum bitrate policy based on a fictitious content publisher's rules.
444.                     switch (protection)
445.                     {
446.                         case HdcpProtection.OnWithTypeEnforcement:
447.                             // Allow full bitrate.
448.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[bitrates.Count - 1];
449.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "full bitrate allowed";
450.                             break;
451.                         case HdcpProtection.On:
452.                             // When there is no HDCP Type 1, make the highest bitrate unavailable.
453.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[bitrates.Count - 2];
454.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "highest bitrate is unavailable";
455.                             break;
456.                         case HdcpProtection.Off:
457.                         case null:
458.                         default:
459.                             // When there is no HDCP at all (Off), or the system is still trying to determine what
460.                             // HDCP protection level to apply (null), then make only the lowest bitrate available.
461.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[0];
462.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "lowest bitrate only";
463.                             break;
464.                     }
465.                     Log($"Imposed DesiredMaxBitrate={ams.DesiredMaxBitrate} for HdcpProtection.{protection}");

JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : value);\n        }\n\n        public JsonObject GetJsonObject()\n        {\n            return container;\n        }\n\n        public override string ToString()\n        {\n            return container.ToString();\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## STEP 4: Create LogView.xaml\n\nCreate `C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run5\\000_AdaptiveStreaming\\app\\AdaptiveStreaming\\Logging\\LogView.xaml` with this content:\n\n```xml\n<!--\n//*********************************************************\n//\n// Copyright (c) Microsoft. Windows.ApplicationModel.DataTransfer;\nusing Microsoft.UI;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;\n\nnamespace SDKTemplate.Logging\n{\n    public enum LogViewLoggingLevel\n    {\n        Verbose = 0,\n        Information,\n        Warning,\n        Error,\n        Critical,\n        Always,\n    }\n\n    public sealed partial class LogView : UserControl\n    {\n        public LogView()\n        {\n            this.InitializeComponent();\n\n            LoggingBrushes = new SolidColorBrush[] {\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"VerboseLogBrush\"],\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"InformationLogBrush\"],\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"WarningLogBrush\"],\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"ErrorLogBrush\"],\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"CriticalLogBrush\"],\n                (SolidColorBrush)Resources[\"AlwaysLogBrush\"],\n            };\n\n            LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)LoggingLevel;\n        }\n\n        public static readonly DependencyProperty LogLevelProperty =\n            DependencyProperty.Register(\"LoggingLevel\", typeof(LogViewLoggingLevel), typeof(LogView),\n                new PropertyMetadata(LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning, OnLoggingLevelChanged));\n\n        public LogViewLoggingLevel LoggingLevel\n        {\n            get { return (LogViewLoggingLevel)GetValue(LogLevelProperty); }\n            set { SetValue(LogLevelProperty, value); }\n        }\n\n        private static void OnLoggingLevelChanged(DependencyObject d, DependencyPropertyChangedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            var logger = (LogView)d;\n            var loggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)e.NewValue;\n            logger.LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)loggingLevel;\n        }\n\n        bool IsLogViewFull = false;\n        int MaxItemsInLogView = 500;\n\n        SolidColorBrush[] LoggingBrushes;\n\n        private void LogLevel_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            LoggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex;\n        }\n\n        public void Log(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level = LogViewLoggingLevel.Always)\n        {\n            DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>\n            {\n                LogFromUIThread(message, level);\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void LogFromUIThread(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level)\n        {\n            string logEntry = $\"{DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss.fff} - {message}\";\n\n            if (LoggingLevel <= level)\n            {\n                SolidColorBrush foregroundBrush = LoggingBrushes[(int)level];\n\n                if (IsLogViewFull)\n                {\n                    System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);\n                    return;\n                }\n\n                if (LoggingListBox.Items.Count >= MaxItemsInLogView)\n                {\n                    IsLogViewFull = true;\n\n                    System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);\n                    logEntry = \"Log window is full\";\n                    foregroundBrush = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);\n                }\n\n                LoggingListBox.Items.Insert(0, new TextBlock() { Text = logEntry, Foreground = foregroundBrush });\n            }\n            else if (level != LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose)\n            {\n                System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine($\"{level}: {logEntry}\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void CopyLogToClipboard_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            var content = new DataPackage();\n            content.SetText(String.Join(\"\\r\\n\", LoggingListBox.Items.Select(item => (item as TextBlock).Text)));\n            Clipboard.SetContent(content);\n        }\n\n        private void ClearLog_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            ClearLog();\n        }\n\n        public void ClearLog()\n        {\n            DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>\n            {\n                LoggingListBox.Items.Clear();\n                IsLogViewFull = false;\n            });\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## STEP 6: Fix threading TODOs in Controls\\ContentSelector.xaml.cs\n\nThe file is at: For Scenario2, Scenario4 - the `DownloadBitrateChanged` and `PlaybackBitrateChanged` event handlers call `await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(...)` etc. Since these are now void methods, change those awaits to plain calls. Also the event handlers themselves were `async void` - they can stay as `async void` or be changed to `void` depending on if there are other awaits. Check carefully.\n\n3. For Scenario5_Metadata.xaml.cs - the method containing migrate-013 and migrate-014 is complex async code. We need to handle the fact that migrate-013 originally captured `currentPosition` via the dispatcher. After migrating to TryEnqueue (fire-and-forget), the `currentPosition` variable assignment won't be awaited. This is a subtlety - read the actual code at lines 540-590 carefully before making changes.\n\n4. Check that after changing UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync and UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync to non-async void methods, the callers in LoadSourceFromUriAsync that were doing `await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(...)` and `await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(...)` are updated to plain calls.\n\n5. The Scenario5 migrate-013 change is tricky - the original code used Dispatcher.RunAsync to get `currentPosition` from UI thread. After the change, TryEnqueue is fire-and-forget so the code after will execute before the lambda. This means the variable `currentPosition` won't be set when the code after needs it. In this case, we should access `mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position` directly (media player position can be accessed from any thread). So replace the whole migrate-013 block with a direct access:\n```csharp\nTimeSpan currentPosition = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position;\n```\n\n6. Similarly for migrate-014, using TryEnqueue to call PlayBreak is fire-and-forget which is fine since it's a UI action.\n\nPlease do all the work carefully and verify the build succeeds. Report all errors and fixes made."}

149.         private void ForceEquirectangularSphericalVideoFrameFormat()
150.         {
151.             if (mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.None ||
152.                 mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.Opening)
153.                 return;
154. 
155.             var mpsvp = mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SphericalVideoProjection;
156.             if (mpsvp == null)
157.                 return;
158.

160.                 mpsvp.FrameFormat = Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular;
161. 
162.             if (mpsvp.IsEnabled != true)
163.                 mpsvp.IsEnabled = true;
164. 
165.             mpsvp.HorizontalFieldOfViewInDegrees = 120;

39.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
42.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
43.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;

57.         {
58.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
59.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
60.             if (mpItem != null)
61.             {
62.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
63.             }
64.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
65.             if (source != null)
66.             {
67.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
68.             }
69. 
70.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
71.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
72.

74.             mediaSourceLogger = null;

82.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             // Explicitly create the instance of MediaPlayer if you need to register for its events
85.             // (like MediaOpened / MediaFailed) prior to setting an IMediaPlaybackSource.
86.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
87. 
88.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
89.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
90. 
91.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
92.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
93.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
94.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // This Scenario focuses on event handling, mostly via the "Logger" helper classes
97.             // in the Shared folder.
98.             //
99.             // In addition, the App can also insert an IHttpFilter into the Windows.Web.Http stack
100.             // which is used by the AdaptiveMediaSource.  To do so, it must derive a class from
101.             // IHttpFilter, provide the HttpBaseProtocolFilter to that class to execute requests,
102.             // then use the derived filter as the constructor for the HttpClient.
103.             //
104.             // The HttpClient is then used in the constructor of the AdaptiveMediaSource.
105.             // The App should explicitly set:
106.             //   CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache
107.             // When passing an HttpClient into the constructor of AdpativeMediaSource,
108.             // set this on the HttpBaseProtocolFilter.
109.             //
110.             // More than one IHttpFilter can be nested, for example, an App might
111.             // use a filter to modify web requests: see Scenario3 for details.
112.             //
113.             // In this scenario, we add an AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger to provide additional
114.             // verbose logging details for each request.
115.             var baseProtocolFilter = new HttpBaseProtocolFilter();
116.             baseProtocolFilter.CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache;  // Always set WriteBehavior = NoCache
117.             var fistLevelFilter = new AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LoggerControl, baseProtocolFilter);
118.             var httpClient = new HttpClient(fistLevelFilter);
119. 
120. 
121.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
122.                 mediaPlayer,
123.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
124.                 httpClient,
125.                 LoggerControl,
126.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
127.         }

132.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
133.         {
134.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
135. 
136.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
137.             if (httpClient != null)
138.             {
139.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
140.             }
141.             else
142.             {
143.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
144.             }
145. 
146.             MediaSource source;
147.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
148.             {
149.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
150. 
151.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
152.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
153. 
154.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
155.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
156. 
157.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
158.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
159. 
160.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource chooses initial playback and download bitrates.
161.                 // See the Tuning scenario for examples of customizing these bitrates.
162.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
163.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
164. 
165.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
166. 
167.             }
168.             else
169.             {
170.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
171. 
172.                 // In this scenario, we make a second attempt to load any URI that is
173.                 // not adaptive streaming as a progressive download URI:
174.                 Log($"Attempting to create a MediaSource from uri: {uri}");
175.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromUri(uri);
176.             }
177. 
178.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
179.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
180. 
181.             // You can save additional information in the CustomPropertySet for future retrieval.
182.             // Note: MediaSource.CustomProperties is a ValueSet and therefore can store
183.             // only serializable types.
184. 
185.             // Save the original Uri.
186.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
187. 
188.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
189.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
190. 
191.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
192. 
193.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
194.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
195. 
196.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
197. 
198.             return mpItem;
199.         }

34.     /// See the README.md for discussion of this scenario.
35.     ///
36.     /// Note: We register but do not unregister event handlers in this scenario, see the EventHandler
37.     ///       scenario for patterns that can be used to clean up.
38.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_RequestModification : Page
39.     {
40. 
41.         private AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod tokenMethod;
42.         private CancellationTokenSource ctsForAppHttpClientForKeys = new CancellationTokenSource();
43. 
44.         public Scenario3_RequestModification()
45.         {
46.             this.InitializeComponent();
47.         }
48. 
49.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
50.         {
51.             // Cancel any HTTP requests the app was making:
52.             ctsForAppHttpClientForKeys.Cancel();
53. 
54.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
55.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
56.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
57.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
58.             {
59.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
60.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
61. 
62.                 UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);
63. 
64.                 mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
65.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
66.             }
67. 
68.             // We clean up HDCP explicitly. Otherwise, the OutputProtectionManager
69.             // will continue to impose our policy until it is finalized by the
70.             // garbage collector.
71.             hdcpSession?.Dispose();
72.             hdcpSession = null;
73.         }
74. 
75.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
76.         {
77.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
78.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
79.             if (mpItem != null)
80.             {
